So the default mode records nothing at all — no ticket, no ledger entry, no plan file, no lock — and prints the decision, the whole menu, and the exact command act mode would have run (`commandFor` / `describeCommand` are one function so the two cannot drift). One wrinkle: with no ticket written, `diagnose` is an illegal move, so the manager's only legal answer at the volume-15 shape would be `escalate` — a correct reading of a state whose honest answer is a question. Watch mode therefore shows the question `--open-ticket` *would* write as a record whose status literally reads `PREVIEW — not written`, which makes the rehearsal mean what it claims while writing nothing. Related, and cheap to get wrong: `answer` must say **which** question it answers when a ticket has more than one open, because `recordAnswer` matches the team's wording exactly and `diagnose.js` refuses to guess — so the loop passes the ticket's own text, not the model's paraphrase, and the gate refuses a paraphrase that matches nothing instead of spawning a command it knows will exit 2.

    **On this machine a manager that answers nothing is a container problem, not a model problem.** Every local container advertises the same model id (`local`) on one shared port, so `harness.assertModelServing()` cannot tell them apart; the only record of which one the `pre-manager` hook started is `hooks/.model-switch-state`. A manager turn served by the translation container answers with nothing. So `managerDecision`'s failure message names that suspicion out loud rather than reporting "the model returned no content" and leaving a human to find it in `.logs/` (gotcha 22, gotcha 62) — and the hooks fire around the role's **own** turn (`runTurnWithHooks(MANAGER_TASK, …)` inside `utils/manager.js`, `utils/diagnostics.js`, `utils/devteam.js`), never around a CLI that merely *might* reach the model, because a switch costs a model load and a CLI cannot know without repeating every refusal the module makes.


## 11. Conventions



- **JSDoc on every function** (params + returns), with provenance comments where a behavior exists because of a live incident ("observed live: …"). New code without JSDoc is a review blocker. Use named types from `types.js` (e.g. `{GlossaryVolumeCtx}` instead of `{Object}`) — the type annotations enable IDE cross-references across files.
- **Update AGENTS.md after changes.** If your work adds, removes, or significantly modifies files, functions, or conventions, update this document to reflect the new state. Agents reading AGENTS.md should be able to rely on it as a current map of the codebase — not a stale one.
- Errors **fail loudly** with actionable messages (pointing at files, `.env` keys, or `.logs/`).
- Prompt files stay mode-agnostic; mode-specific text is appended in code (`AGENT_TOOLS_NOTE`), never forked into separate prompt files.
- Tests: pure logic in `test/test-glossary-load.js` (incl. the acceptance reply parsing, `truncateGlossary`'s relevance window, the coverage→extraction feedback note, the seeded-vs-unseeded glossary write instruction and the recovery prompt that must NOT demand a whole-file rewrite, the compact glossary index and its honest cap note, `compareGlossaryCarryForward` (loss, reorder-is-not-loss, no baseline, **alias-resolution: widening a row with a new alias is not a loss, splitting one alias row into several is not a loss, dropping one spelling out of an alias row IS a loss**, **rename-resolution: a term carried under a new source-language spelling is not a loss — recorded in the row's Notes, or proven by a unique matching rendering; a renamed row is not counted as an addition; a deleted entry with no trace still fails; an incidental mention in a row that is already carrying a term does not excuse a deletion; a one-character spelling is never proven by substring matching**), the three scaled step caps, **the cumulative-document rules now in all three cumulative stages** (`voicePrimaryName` / `parseVoiceSections` / `compareVoiceCarryForward` — a reworded character heading is not a lost character, a dropped persona is; `voiceWriteInstruction` seeded-vs-unseeded and `voiceRecoveryPrompt` refusing to demand the whole-file rewrite; `buildVoiceIndex` and its honest cap note; the compile and feedback turns carrying the edit-in-place instruction and the batched source checks; `parseStyleSections` / `countStyleRules` / `compareStyleCarryForward` — a a step not on the menu and an unparseable reply both stop the loop and print the menu it was offered, the unparseable one naming `model-switch-state`; `end` accepted on a clean fixture and refused on a broken one; act mode writes the triage's question **once** and then offers it as a `diagnose` move, and a second loop run does not duplicate it; an accept the machine cannot vouch for stops the loop without spawning anything and leaves the patch `proposed`; a live run lock held by somebody else's run id refuses before spending a decision; the iteration cap firing on legal-but-non-repeating moves while the answers it made are real records; and the CLI refusing `--dry-run`, a mode that does not exist, an unknown flag, a missing `SERIES_LOCATION` and a series folder that does not exist — no scenario spawns a pipeline step, and `test/fixture-series.js` is the shared builder both this suite and `test/test-resume.js` use so a fixture cannot drift from `utils/artifacts.js`, see gotcha 76) — all plain `assert`, no framework (keep it that way); live behavior in `test/harness-smoke.js`.
- **Two levels of stubbing — pick the one that matches what you are testing.** (a) *Replace the harness call*: `harness.runOneShot = async () => "…"` / `harness.createAgentHandle = async () => fakeHandle` (see `test/test-qa-orchestration.js`, `test/test-task-failure.js`, `test/test-wiki-orchestration.js`). Cheap, precise, and the right tool for task logic — the task modules all call THROUGH the `harness` module object, so the patch applies. But it cuts the harness out: no provider, no streaming parser, no agent loop, no tool schemas, no approve gate, no retry, no truncation guard, no size tagging. (b) *Replace the model server*: `test/fake-backend.js`, and point `AI_BASE_URL` / `<ROLE>_BASE_URL` at it. Everything below the task code then runs for real, including the failure shapes (`backend.fail("truncated")`, `"tooBig"`, `"serverError"`, `"hang"`, …). Use (b) whenever the thing you are testing lives in the harness or in the wire contract, and whenever a test claims to cover a "live-only" phase (gotcha 49) — a scripted endpoint reaches every phase a dry run cannot. **To run the whole pipeline offline**, put `test/fake-workflow.js` behind (b) and drive it with `test/test-pipeline-loop.js`: the brain dispatches on the request itself (which system prompt, which tools were advertised, how far into the agent turn the message history already is) because the label is not on the wire. Then read `test/prompt-audit.js` — it audits what the pipeline ASKED, not only what it produced, which is the half a green run cannot tell you: a run can pass every gate and still be answering a prompt that never contained the glossary.
- **Grader calibration** (`test/calibration/` + `npm run calibrate`) — the quality system rests on a number a model produces, and this measures whether the number means anything. Six cases, each a short source text with a KNOWN defect in one translation (an omission, a meaning flip, a terminology break, a POV erasure + misattribution, an invented event, a quiet tense/duration drift) and a faithful rendering in the other. `test/calibrate.js` grades both with the PRODUCTION verifier (`gradeChapter` + `buildVerifyPrompt` + the production rubric and temperature — not an imitation of it) and reports, per case, the score, the band, and the gap, plus the distribution and the drift since the previous run. It exits non-zero when a grader ranks a known defect at or above the passing line, or cannot tell a flawed text from a faithful one. LIVE (it calls the `VERIFY_*` endpoint), so it is NOT part of `npm test`. The fixture is honest in both directions: the first run caught a real mistranslation in the fixture author's own "clean" text.
- Dependencies: AI SDK v6 + `@openharness/core` v0.7 + `jszip`/`cheerio` (epub extraction in `utils/source.js`); keep CommonJS, no new frameworks.

## 13. Ownership
You are the owner of this repository. all code in this repository is written by you and other agents like you. Do not assume that the user understand anything about the inner workings of this project nor should you assume that anything you find in this project was written by the user. The features in this project are added, maintained, and managed entirely by AI agents and the user acts as a client who requests features which you implmement. 

## 13. Communication

When communicating with the user, you should assume that the user is intelegent but not knowledgeable. As mentioned earlier, you are the owner of hte code in this repo, as such, you should not expect any particular terms found in the code or the comments to be understood by the user. When formulating your final response, keep the following points in mind as you produce your final answer.
- Avoid using advanced jargon and specialized terminolgy where possible.
- Just because a term or jargon is used in the code doesn't mean the user udnerstands the meaning of those words.
- Ask yourself "Would a general audiance software engineering influencer/educator use these terms?" If not, then you should probably avoid the use of the jargon in question.
- Ask yourself "How likely would a term appear in a PhD research paper?" If the odds are high, it's probably best to avoid using the jargon in question.
- When you are producing your final answer, instead of using specialized jargon, you can use analogies or metaphors instead.
- When analogies or metaphors fail, psuedo code can be used as a last resort.
